<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that the centered uniform odd integer height function on triangular-lattice approximations of a smooth simply connected domain, with neighboring differences zero or two and boundary values +1 and −1 on two arcs, converges to a universal multiple of the Dirichlet Gaussian free field. This resolves the field part of Schramm's Problem 2.2. We give an absolutely convergent finite-volume formula for the normalization. The proof combines reflection positivity, a spectral sum rule, and boundary comparison with Gaussian moment identities.

- **claim-main-gff-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $D$ be a bounded smooth simply connected planar domain with two distinct marked boundary points. Let $D_\delta$ be simply connected triangular-lattice polygonal approximations with uniformly converging marked boundary parametrizations and edge-midpoint marks, and let $h_\delta$ have the uniform odd-height law <a href="#eq:odd-model" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:odd-model">[eq:odd-model]</a>. There is a constant $\sigma>0$, depending only on the unit triangular-lattice model, such that $$h_\delta\ \Longrightarrow\ \bar g+\sigma\Phi_D \qquad\text{in }\mathcal D'(D).$$ For every $f\in C_c^\infty(D)$, $\mathbb Eh_\delta(f)\to\int_D\bar g f$. Consequently $$\label{eq:main-centered} \frac{h_\delta-\mathbb Eh_\delta}{\sigma} \ \Longrightarrow\ \Phi_D \qquad\text{in }\mathcal D'(D).$$ All finite vectors of test-function integrals converge jointly to the corresponding Gaussian vectors, and all their mixed moments converge. The constant is specified by the absolutely convergent finite-volume series in <a href="#prop:normalization-series" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:normalization-series">31</a>; in the spectral convention below, $\sigma=4\pi\sqrt{c_*}$.
  - Explanation: The centered odd-height field on fine triangular-lattice approximations converges to the harmonic extension of the two-arc data plus a universal multiple of the Dirichlet Gaussian free field, with joint test-function limits and moments.
- **claim-gray-lift-loops** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-gray)): On a simply connected triangular domain, a coherent pair determines integer height increments $$\label{eq:height-increment} dH(x,y)=\frac{W_xB_y-B_xW_y}{2}.$$ They have zero curl. Fixing the height at one vertex gives a bijection between coherent pairs whose Gray state there matches that prescribed integer and admissible heights with that value. Uniform counting has the height Gibbs property.* *With a constant boundary height, the nonzero height steps form disjoint closed honeycomb loops. Conditional on their geometry, the inward jumps are independent fair signs; the unoriented loop weight is $2^{\#\mathrm{loops}}$. The same conclusion holds in a bounded face of a connected skeleton of fixed lattice edges carrying one constant height.
  - Explanation: Coherent spin pairs give curl-free integer height increments, a bijection with admissible heights and the height Gibbs property, and constant boundary data give a loop representation with independent fair signs.
- **claim-spin-fkg** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-spin-fkg)): The $B$ marginal of $\mu^{E_0,\eta}$ satisfies the FKG lattice condition. The assertion remains true after any further $B$ pinnings. In particular, fixing additional $B$ spins to $+1$ increases increasing events, and fixing them to $-1$ decreases such events.* *Conditional on $B$, the spin $W$ consists of independent fair signs on the components of the graph generated by $E_0$ and the $B$-changing edges.
  - Explanation: The first-spin marginal under second-color equality constraints satisfies the FKG lattice condition, also after further pinnings, so extra plus or minus pins act monotonically; the second spin is a set of independent fair signs on components.
- **claim-double-cut-law** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-double-cut)): Let $C$ be a simple double-$B$ circuit of sign $t$. Suppose that no edge of $E_0$ crosses $C$. Conditional on the exterior pair configuration and on the $B=t$ values on the inner boundary layer of $C$, the interior law is the uniform coherent-pair law with that $B$ boundary layer, free $W$ boundary values, and just the constraints situated inside $C$.* *This assertion also holds for a circuit selected by a stopping exploration which reveals only exterior $B$ spins and the $B=t$ cut sites. No $W$ spins need be revealed during that exploration. Along the inner boundary face chain, a height lift takes values in one pair of consecutive integers.
  - Explanation: Inside a double circuit with no crossing equality edges, the conditional interior law is the uniform coherent-pair law with the given first-spin layer and free second spin, also for circuits found by a stopping exploration.
- **claim-local-double-circuits** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-local-circuit)): There are $R>3$, $p>0$, and $r_0<\infty$ with the following property. In a constrained coherent-pair law, suppose that the radius-$Rr$ face-ball patch about $z$, including the two layers of its bounding contour, contains no prescribed spins or $E_0$ edges. For $r\ge r_0$, conditional on arbitrary $B$ values outside that patch, there is probability at least $p$ of a simple double-$B$ circuit of either specified sign whose curve surrounds $z$ and lies at norm radii $$\label{eq:local-circuit-annulus} (1-\varepsilon)r\le \|\,\cdot-z\|_g\le(2+\varepsilon)r.$$ The circuit encloses the real norm ball of radius $(1-\varepsilon)r$. For a sign-$t$ circuit, additional $B=t$ pins in the patch are allowed.
  - Explanation: In an unconstrained face-ball patch a double circuit of either sign surrounding the center exists with probability bounded below, uniformly over exterior conditioning.
- **claim-worst-cut-circuit** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-worst-cut)): There are fixed $K<\infty$ and $c>0$ with the following property. Inside a regular dual cut of radius $Kn$, fix $B=-t$ on the inner boundary layer and leave $W$ free. With probability at least $c$, there is a double-$B$ circuit of sign $t$ between radii $n$ and $2n$. The constants are independent of $n$, for all sufficiently large $n$.
  - Explanation: Even with the opposite sign imposed on the inner boundary layer, a double circuit of the desired sign appears between two radii with probability bounded below independently of the scale.
- **claim-buffer-circuits** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-buffer-circuits)): Consider a fixed positive-width polygonal collar, or a fixed positive-width tube following a polygonal arc. Suppose it has a buffer disjoint from $E_0$ and from all prescribed $B$ spins. At sufficiently fine mesh, conditional on arbitrary $B$ values away from the buffer, there is a probability at least $c>0$ of a double-$B$ circuit around the collar’s hole, or a connected double-$B$ path following the tube, of either specified sign. For a sign-$t$ event, arbitrary additional $B=t$ pins in the buffer are allowed. The constant depends on the fixed geometry, not on the mesh, the number of pins elsewhere, or their values.* *Finitely many separated such requirements have a uniformly positive joint probability, even if different buffers request different signs. For a logarithmic stack of separated similar annular buffers between radii $r$ and $L$, the probability that no circuit of a prescribed sign occurs is at most $$\label{eq:stack-failure} C(r/L)^\alpha$$ for some $\alpha>0$. In lattice units this holds uniformly for $L\gg r\geq1$, after adjustment of $C$.
  - Explanation: Collars and tubes with a pin-free buffer carry double circuits or paths of either sign with probability bounded below, jointly for finitely many buffers, and a logarithmic stack fails only with a power-law small probability.
- **claim-loop-estimates** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-loop-estimates)): There are constants $c_0>0$ and $0<\rho<1$ such that, for all sufficiently large integer $n$, the following statements hold.* 1. *If the interior of a simple contour $L$ contains $\Lambda_{2n}(z)$, then $\mathbb P_L$ gives probability at least $c_0$ to a loop contained in $\Lambda_{2n}(z)$ and surrounding $\Lambda_n(z)$.* 2. *If $L$ has graph inradius $n$ about $z$, then $$\label{eq:upper-loop-estimate} \mathbb P_L\bigl(\text{at least two loops surround }\Lambda_{\rho n}(z)\bigr) \le 1-c_0.$$* *There is no aspect-ratio or boundary-regularity assumption on $L$.
  - Explanation: In any simple contour there is a surrounding loop with probability bounded below, and two nested loops around a contracted ball occur with probability bounded away from one, without regularity assumptions.
- **claim-plane-mixing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-plane-mixing)): Free coherent pairs in expanding regular hexagons converge on cylinder events to a law $\mu_\infty$. This law is invariant under lattice translations, lattice symmetries, global sign changes, and interchange of the colors. If the domain of a finite pair law $\mu^{E_0,\eta}$ contains a ball of radius $L$ and has no constraints in that ball, then its restriction to a concentric ball of radius $r$ differs from that of $\mu_\infty$ in total variation by at most $C(r/L)^\alpha$, for $L\gg r\geq1$.* *The associated plane height increments exist and have the same spatial symmetries. Every finite feasible prescription of $B$ pins together with $W=+1$ on finitely many connected paths has positive plane probability.
  - Explanation: Free coherent pairs in growing hexagons converge to a symmetric plane law, and a finite law with an empty ball agrees with it on a smaller ball up to a power-law total variation error; finite feasible pin events have positive plane probability.
- **claim-relative-pin-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-relative-pin-comparison)): Let $\mu$ and $\nu$ be finite pair laws with second-color equality constraints and first-color pins. Their domains contain the same filled isolating neighborhood $U$, and their constraints inside $U$ agree: these consist of retained $B$ pins $R$ and equality edges $E_0$ on one obstacle. Its surrounding collar and buffers in $U$ contain no constraints. Outside $U$ the two laws may have different constraints. If $A$ prescribes one sign $t$ on further sites of that obstacle, consistently with $R$, then $$c\nu(A)\leq\mu(A)\leq c^{-1}\nu(A).$$ Here $c>0$ depends only on the collar and its buffers, not on the retained pins, equality edges, remote constraints, or the probability of $A$. The same assertion holds with the colors interchanged.
  - Explanation: Probabilities of further single-sign pins on one obstacle are comparable under two laws that agree inside an isolating neighborhood, with a constant depending only on the collar geometry.
- **claim-pin-product** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-pin-product)): For a separated family as above, there are constants $0<c\leq C<\infty$ such that $$\label{eq:pin-product} c\prod_i p_i\ \leq\ \mu_\infty\Bigl(\bigcap_iP_i\Bigr) \ \leq\ C\prod_i p_i.$$ The constants depend on the collars, their buffers, the number of obstacles, and $\sum_i m_i$. They do not depend on the mesh, the number or the values of the microscopic $B$ pins, or the probabilities $p_i$. The same estimates hold in all sufficiently large finite free volumes.
  - Explanation: For separated obstacles the joint plane probability of the pin events is comparable to the product of the individual probabilities, with constants independent of mesh and microscopic pins.
- **claim-midpoint-tail-amplification** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-midpoint-tails)): Let $\mathcal S$ be a finite set of integer arrays with its uniform law, let $Y$ be a real linear statistic, and let $M>0$. Partition $\mathcal S$ into at most $m_*$ classes. Suppose that whenever $x,y$ belong to the same class, both coordinatewise rounded midpoints belong to $\mathcal S$, and that their $Y$ values differ from $(Y(x)+Y(y))/2$ by at most $M$. Suppose also that, for every class in use, $$\label{eq:class-central-mass} \mathbb P\{\abs Y\leq M,\ x\hbox{ belongs to that class}\}\geq c>0.$$ Then, for constants depending only on $c$ and $m_*$, $$\mathbb P(\abs Y>tM)\leq C e^{-c't}\quad(t\geq1),\qquad \norm{Y}_{L^p}\leq C_pM\quad(p<\infty).$$ The rounded outputs need not belong to the input class.
  - Explanation: A central probability bound on a linear statistic of integer arrays, together with rounded-midpoint admissibility within classes, yields exponential tails and bounded moments of every order.
- **claim-loop-shell-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-loop-shells)): In a finite flat-boundary height problem, fix an interior site $x$. The number of level loops surrounding $x$ whose graph inradii lie between $d$ and $2d$ has moments bounded at every order, uniformly in the domain and in $d\geq1$. The same holds when the shell is defined by Euclidean closest approach to $x$. Both assertions also hold in a bounded face of a connected constant-height skeleton.
  - Explanation: The number of level loops around a site in a dyadic distance shell has moments of every order bounded uniformly in the domain and the radius.
- **claim-interior-smearing-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-smearing-moments)): Consider a uniform height problem with boundary values bounded in absolute value by $K$. Let the center of a radius-$a$ smearing have distance $n$ from the boundary, with $1\leq a\leq c_0n$ for a sufficiently small fixed $c_0>0$. Then, for every finite $p$, $$\label{eq:smearing-moments} \norm{\textstyle\sum_xw_xH(x)}_{L^p} \leq C_p\bigl(1+\sqrt{\log(n/a)}\bigr).$$ Here $C_p$ depends on $p,A,K$, but not on boundary regularity. The same statement holds for bounded data on the inner face chain of a dual cut, and in the bounded skeleton faces considered above.
  - Explanation: Smooth averages of the height at a distance from the boundary have moments growing at most like the square root of the logarithm of the distance-to-radius ratio, independently of boundary regularity.
- **claim-plane-zero-total-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-plane-moments)): For every zero-total smearing satisfying <a href="#eq:normalized-weights" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:normalized-weights">[eq:normalized-weights]</a>, $$\label{eq:plane-zero-total-moments} \norm{\textstyle\sum_x w_xH(x)}_{L^p(\mu_\infty)}\leq C_p, \qquad p<\infty,$$ uniformly in its radius, location, and lattice rotation. The same bound holds in sufficiently larger finite free volumes, for a height anchored outside the construction.
  - Explanation: Zero-total lattice smearings have moments of all finite orders bounded uniformly in radius, location and rotation under the plane law and in large free volumes.
- **claim-component-switch** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-component-switch)): Suppose that two nested double-$B$ circuits have opposite signs, and that their intervening collar contains no $E_0$ edge or pinned $W$ site. There is a component of $B$-changing edges confined to this collar whose fair $W$ sign can be flipped so as to change every height inside the inner cut by $4$ in absolute value relative to every height outside the outer cut. The flip changes no spin pin outside the collar. It preserves any inner height prescription invariant under a common additive shift in $4\mathbb Z$, while leaving exterior heights fixed.* *Conditional on $B$ and the other $W$ component labels, the two choices are equally likely. If a zero-total statistic has total weight $\lambda$ inside the inner cut, no weight in the collar, and all its other weight outside the outer cut, its two values differ by $4\abs\lambda$ and its conditional variance is at least $4\lambda^2$.
  - Explanation: Between two nested opposite-sign double circuits a fair component sign can be flipped, shifting every inner height by a fixed amount relative to the outside and giving a variance lower bound for zero-total statistics.
- **claim-obstacle-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-obstacle-moments)): Fix finitely many separated interval-compatible obstacles with filled isolating neighborhoods $U_i$ as in <a href="#prop:pin-product" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:pin-product">12</a>. Let a test disk lie in a pin-free simply connected neighborhood $U_0$, with room for a surrounding buffered cut whose filled interior lies in $U_0$. Require $\overline U_0$ to be disjoint from every $\overline U_i$. Let $Y$ be a zero-total smearing in the test disk, satisfying <a href="#eq:normalized-weights" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:normalized-weights">[eq:normalized-weights]</a> at its scale. Conditional on all the pins, $$\label{eq:obstacle-moment-bound} \norm Y_{L^p}\leq C_p\qquad(p<\infty).$$ The constants depend on the macroscopic arrangement, the weight bound, the number of obstacles, and the bound on W-subpath counts, but not on the mesh or the microscopic pin probabilities. No uniformity as obstacle separations vanish is asserted here.
  - Explanation: Conditional on all pins of separated interval-compatible obstacles, a zero-total smearing in a pin-free test disk has moments of every order bounded independently of the mesh and the microscopic pin probabilities.
- **claim-equal-offset-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-equal-offset-moments)): For a nonempty obstacle family in the preceding setup, impose the additional event $\mathcal E=\{t_i=t_1\hbox{ for every }i\}$. The obstacles may now vary or approach one another, while staying away from a fixed test disk. Fix an annular buffer around that disk, with positive clearance from the disk and with fixed inner and outer radii. Suppose the buffer and its filled interior contain no pins. Suppose that, under conditioning on the pins and $\mathcal E$, an exterior search finds a surrounding double-$B$ cut in this buffer, with probability at least $c>0$ uniformly in the varying arrangements. Suppose that $\mathcal E$ can be checked in the exterior of the selected cut, so its strict interior remains free given the trace. Then the zero-total test in that disk has moments bounded uniformly in those arrangements.* *A sufficient condition is that the cut probability conditional on the pins alone is at least $c_0>0$, that $\mathbb P(\mathcal E\mid\hbox{pins})\to1$ uniformly along the varying family, and that the same exterior measurability holds.
  - Explanation: Under equal offsets the moment bounds persist uniformly while obstacles vary or approach each other, provided a surrounding cut is found with uniform probability and the offset event is exterior measurable.
- **claim-reflection-positivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-reflection-positivity)): Let $\ell$ be a line of lattice sites parallel to a nearest-neighbor direction. The plane law is reflection positive across $\ell$. This remains true after adjoining any finite collection of phase fields $$\label{eq:auxiliary-phases} Z_\theta(u)=U_\theta\exp\{i\theta(H(u)-H(o))\},\qquad \theta\in\mathbb R,$$ where the $U_\theta$’s are independent uniform points of the unit circle, independent of the pair, and $o$ is any anchor.* *More precisely, let $\Theta_\ell$ denote spatial reflection, and let $\mathcal A_\ell$ consist of cylinder functions supported in one closed half-plane, with increments evaluated along paths in that half-plane. Then $$\langle F,G\rangle_\ell =\mathbb E\big[\overline{\Theta_\ell F}\,G\big]$$ is positive semidefinite. In its quotient completion, normal translation farther into the half-plane by $\sqrt3$ is a positive self-adjoint contraction $S$. Tangential translation by one lattice unit is a commuting unitary operator. On mesh $\delta$, the normal step is $\sqrt3\delta$.
  - Explanation: The plane law is reflection positive across a site row, also with auxiliary Haar phases; normal translation becomes a positive self-adjoint contraction and tangential translation a commuting unitary.
- **claim-increment-spectral** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-increment-spectral)): There is a nonnegative measure $C$, locally finite on $\widehat\mathbb T\setminus\{0\}$, such that $$\label{eq:increment-spectral} \mathbb E\left(\sum_u a_uH(u)\right)^2 =\int_{\widehat\mathbb T\setminus\{0\}} \left\|\sum_u a_ue^{ip\cdot u}\right\|^2 C(dp)$$ for every finitely supported real array with $\sum_u a_u=0$. The measure is invariant under the lattice reflections and rotations. There is no additional random linear tilt term in this identity.
  - Explanation: Variances of zero-sum linear combinations of heights are given by a symmetric nonnegative spectral measure on the punctured dual torus, with no random linear tilt term.
- **claim-annulus-mass** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-annulus-mass)): For some finite constant $K$, $$\label{eq:annulus-mass} C\{r\le \|p\|\le2r\}\le K,\qquad 0<r<1/4.$$ In particular, for every $\eta>0$, $\int_{0<\|p\|<1/4}\|p\|^\eta C(dp)<\infty$.
  - Explanation: The spectral measure of dyadic annuli near the origin is uniformly bounded, so powers of the frequency are integrable against it.
- **claim-cauchy-mixture** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-cauchy-mixture)): There is a nonnegative measure $\nu(dk,ds)$ on $(\mathbb R/2\pi\mathbb Z\setminus\{0\})\times[0,\infty]$, finite on sets whose horizontal projection stays away from zero, such that $$\label{eq:cauchy-mixture} C'(dk,dl)=\int_{[0,\infty]}Q_s(dl)\,\nu(dk,ds),\qquad k\ne0.$$ Moreover, $$\label{eq:weighted-nu} \int_{0<\|k\|<\kappa}\|k\|\,\nu(dk,ds)<\infty$$ for every sufficiently small fixed $\kappa>0$.
  - Explanation: Away from zero horizontal frequency the projected spectral measure is a positive mixture of periodized Cauchy kernels, with a weighted integrability bound for the mixing measure near zero.
- **claim-angular-poisson** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-angular-poisson)): Uniformly for $s\in[0,\infty]$ and $0<\|k\|<\kappa$, $$\label{eq:angular-poisson} \int\chi(k,l)Q_s(dl) =D(k,s)+O(\|k\|),\qquad D(k,s)=\left(\frac{\|k\|-s}{\|k\|+s}\right)^2,$$ where $D(k,\infty)=1$.
  - Explanation: The vertical Poisson average of the fourth angular harmonic equals a nonnegative dispersion defect up to a lattice periodization error that is linear in the horizontal frequency.
- **claim-dispersion-defect** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-dispersion-defect)): For some fixed $\kappa>0$, $$\label{eq:dispersion-defect} \int_{0<\|k\|<\kappa}D(k,s)\,\nu(dk,ds)<\infty.$$ There is a finite constant $K_1$ such that $$\label{eq:horizontal-band-mass} \nu\{r\le\|k\|\le2r\}\le K_1$$ for all sufficiently small $r>0$.
  - Explanation: The dispersion defect is integrable against the mixing measure near zero and horizontal dyadic bands carry uniformly bounded mixing mass.
- **claim-endcap** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-endcap)): For a finite constant $K_3$, $$\label{eq:endcap} C\{r<\|p\|<2r,\ \|k\|\le\varepsilon\}\le K_3\frac{\varepsilon}{r}, \qquad 0<\varepsilon<r/10,\quad 0<r<1/4.$$ In particular the punctured small disk has no mass on the line $k=0$.
  - Explanation: The spectral mass of thin endcaps near the vertical axis is proportional to their relative width, so the punctured small disk has no mass on the axis.
- **claim-spectral-subsequence-form** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-spectral-subsequences)): Every such vague limit has the form $$\label{eq:spectral-subsequence} M(dp)=c\,\frac{dp}{\|p\|^2}$$ for a finite $c\ge0$, initially allowed to depend on the subsequence.
  - Explanation: Every vague subsequential limit of the rescaled spectral measures is a constant multiple of the inverse-square-radius density on the punctured plane.
- **claim-angular-sum-rule** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-angular-sum)): The series $\sum_{j\ge0}\|I_j\|$ converges. There is a single constant $c_*\ge0$ such that every limit in <a href="#lem:spectral-subsequences" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:spectral-subsequences">27</a> has coefficient $c_*$, and $$\label{eq:angular-sum} \sum_{j\ge0}I_j=-\frac\pi2 c_*.$$ The value is independent of the choice of $q$ satisfying <a href="#eq:cutoff-properties" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="eq:cutoff-properties">[eq:cutoff-properties]</a>.
  - Explanation: The dyadic angular series converges absolutely and its sum fixes one common coefficient for every subsequential limit, independently of the cutoff.
- **claim-plane-covariance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-plane-covariance)): The rescaled spectral measures converge vaguely on the punctured plane to $c_*\,dp/\|p\|^2$, with $0<c_*<\infty$. Put $v=(2\pi)^2c_*$. For real $f,g\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R^2)$ of integral zero, $$\begin{aligned} \label{eq:plane-covariance} \lim_{\delta\downarrow0}\mathbb E[H_\delta(f)H_\delta(g)] &=c_*\int_{\mathbb R^2} \frac{\widehat f(\zeta)\overline{\widehat g(\zeta)}}{\|\zeta\|^2}\,d\zeta \notag\\ &=\frac{v}{2\pi}\iint_{\mathbb R^2\times\mathbb R^2} f(x)\log\frac1{\|x-y\|}\,g(y)\,dx\,dy. \end{aligned}$$ The same conclusion holds for tests converging in $C_c^\infty$ with support in a common compact set.
  - Explanation: The rescaled spectral measures converge to a positive finite multiple of the inverse-square density, and zero-integral smeared heights converge in covariance to the logarithmic kernel with the stated multiplier.
- **claim-reflection-null** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-reflection-null)): Let $\phi\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R^2)$ be supported strictly on one side of a line parallel to a lattice reflection direction. Choose site rows $\ell_\delta$ converging to that line, still separated from the support by a positive margin. For $$\label{eq:laplace-insertion} X_\delta=H_\delta(\Delta\phi),$$ its reflection norm at $\ell_\delta$ tends to zero. This holds in every finite phase augmentation of <a href="#prop:reflection-positivity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:reflection-positivity">20</a>, and also for lattice translations and reflected placements converging to the stated configuration.
  - Explanation: The height paired with the Laplacian of a test function supported on one side of a lattice reflection line has reflection norm tending to zero, even with auxiliary phases; the ordinary variance need not vanish.
- **claim-normalization-series** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-normalization-series)): The series of the indicated terms is absolutely convergent: $$\label{eq:normalization-absolute} \sum_{j\ge0}\|b_{j0}\|+ \sum_{j,s\ge0}\|b_{j,s+1}-b_{js}\|<\infty.$$ Its value satisfies $$\label{eq:finite-normalization} c_*=\frac1\pi\sum_{j\ge0} \left[b_{j0}+\sum_{s\ge0}(b_{j,s+1}-b_{js})\right]>0, \qquad \sigma=4\pi\sqrt{c_*}.$$ Thus the displayed formula specifies a strictly positive universal normalization using only [eq:explicit-cutoff,eq:normalization-multiplier,eq:normalization-fourier,eq:normalization-radii,eq:finite-band-coefficient] [eq:explicit-cutoff](#eq:explicit-cutoff), [eq:normalization-multiplier](#eq:normalization-multiplier), [eq:normalization-fourier](#eq:normalization-fourier), [eq:normalization-radii](#eq:normalization-radii), [eq:finite-band-coefficient](#eq:finite-band-coefficient).
  - Explanation: The normalization constant is given by an absolutely convergent series built from finite-volume spin counts and Fourier coefficients of an explicit cutoff, and is strictly positive.
- **claim-boundary-brackets** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-boundary-brackets)): The boundary traces in these laws satisfy the following actual integer height constraints: $$\label{eq:bracket-intervals} \begin{array}{c\|cc} & \text{off the windows}&\text{on a window}\\ \hline \mu^{+,\mathcal W}_\delta & H=g_\delta&H\in\{0,1\}\\ \mu^{-,\mathcal W}_\delta & H=g_\delta&H\in\{-2,-1\}. \end{array}$$ Conditional on the trace, the interior has the uniform height Gibbs law with that trace. Consequently there is a coupling with the true height law $\mu^g_\delta$ such that $$\label{eq:bracket-order} H^-_\delta\le H^g_\delta\le H^+_\delta \quad\text{at every vertex, and hence after affine interpolation}.$$ The bracket means lie between $-2$ and $1$. The bounded-boundary smearing estimates of <a href="#prop:smearing-moments" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:smearing-moments">15</a> apply to both brackets, uniformly in the sizes and number of windows.
  - Explanation: Pinning only one spin on boundary windows gives upper and lower laws with explicit integer boundary intervals that stochastically sandwich the true height law, with bounded means and smearing estimates.
- **claim-normalized-reflection** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-normalized-reflection)): Suppose the obstacle events $P_1,\ldots,P_m$ and the support of $A$ are strictly on one side of a reflection row. The obstacles have pairwise disjoint filled isolating neighborhoods, and every unbounded insertion in $A$ has a pin-free filled test neighborhood disjoint from them. Choose these neighborhoods inside the same open half-plane. Let $p_i=\mathbb P(P_i)$ and $$V=A\prod_{i=1}^m\frac{\mathbf 1_{P_i}}{p_i}.$$ Assume $A$ is either uniformly bounded or a uniformly bounded function times one smooth zero-total height insertion in such a disk. Bounded factors may include the phase fields <a href="#eq:haar-phase" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:haar-phase">[eq:haar-phase]</a>. Then $\\|V\\|_{\vartheta}\le C$, uniformly as $\delta\downarrow0$. The assertion is uniform when the macroscopic arrangements range over a compact family preserving all the stated positive separations.
  - Explanation: After dividing by the individual pin probabilities, the reflection norm of the pinned observable on one side of a row is bounded uniformly in the mesh.
- **claim-analytic-translation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-analytic-translation)): Partition a finite arrangement of pin obstacles and local factors into two groups strictly separated by projection on a lattice reflection normal $n$. The factors have the form allowed in <a href="#lem:normalized-reflection" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:normalized-reflection">34</a>, with at most one unbounded zero-total insertion in the entire arrangement. Move the high group rigidly by $tn$, keeping the low group fixed, and normalize the plane expectation by the product of the individual obstacle probabilities. Denote this quantity, at lattice normal step translations, by $M_\delta(t)$.* *If $M_\delta(t_\delta)\to0$ for every $t$ in a nonempty open interval $I\subset(0,\infty)$ and lattice-rounded $t_\delta\to t$, then $M_\delta(0)\to0$. The same implication holds with the low group moved in direction $-n$. It also holds along sequences of other fixed placements converging with all the required positive clearances.
  - Explanation: If a normalized expectation vanishes for all translations in an open interval, analytic continuation of transfer powers shows it vanishes at zero translation.
- **claim-gapped-identity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-gapped-identity)): Fix windows, gaps and tests satisfying the preceding conditions. Let $P=\bigcap_{i=1}^mP_i$ and $J_\delta=\prod_{i=1}^m\mathbb P(P_i)$. For any product $\Psi_\delta$ of unit-modulus phase factors at the representatives $v_i$, $$\label{eq:gapped-phase-identity} \frac{\mathbb E[X_\delta F_\delta\Psi_\delta\mathbf 1_P]}{J_\delta} \longrightarrow0.$$ The phases may have arbitrary fixed real frequencies and the independent Haar lifts of <a href="#eq:haar-phase" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:haar-phase">[eq:haar-phase]</a>. The assertion is valid for every convergent sequence of lattice-rounded placements with these strict separations.* *If $E$ is the event that all offsets in <a href="#eq:obstacle-intervals" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:obstacle-intervals">[eq:obstacle-intervals]</a> agree, then also $$\label{eq:gapped-equal-offset-identity} \frac{\mathbb E[X_\delta F_\delta\mathbf 1_{P\cap E}]}{J_\delta} \longrightarrow0.$$
  - Explanation: With separated gaps in the boundary pins, the normalized expectation of the Laplace insertion against the pinned test factors, including phases and the equal-offset event, tends to zero.
- **claim-gap-filling** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-gap-filling)): There are $\beta>0$ and a function $\epsilon(a)\to0$ as $a\downarrow0$ such that $$\label{eq:offset-high-probability} \limsup_{\delta\downarrow0}\mu_{\delta,a}(E^c) \le\epsilon(a).$$ If $\mathscr I_\delta$ is the joint increment information in the fixed test disks, then $$\label{eq:gap-tv} \limsup_{\delta\downarrow0} \big\\|\mu^{=}_{\delta,a}\|_{\mathscr I_\delta} -\mu_{\delta,0}\|_{\mathscr I_\delta}\big\\|_{\rm TV} \le\epsilon(a).$$ For every finite $p$, and for all sufficiently small $a$, the variables $X_\delta$ have uniformly bounded $p$th moments under $\mu^{=}_{\delta,a}$ and under $\mu_{\delta,0}$, in the sense of a bound on $\limsup_{\delta\downarrow0}$ independent of $a$. No such uniform assertion is needed for the law conditioned on $E^c$.
  - Explanation: Offsets agree with high probability once gaps are small, the equal-offset gapped law is close in total variation to the filled law on the test information, and moments of the insertion stay uniformly bounded.
- **claim-bracket-identity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-bracket-identity)): Fix a window system $\mathcal W$ for a finite grid $\mathcal L$, and either bracket from <a href="#lem:boundary-brackets" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:boundary-brackets">33</a>. Let $K_0,\ldots,K_q\Subset D$ be disks, with $K_0$ at positive distance from every $K_j$, $j\ge1$, and let $X_\delta,F_\delta$ be as in <a href="#eq:pins-insertion" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pins-insertion">[eq:pins-insertion]</a>. Suppose there are two levels $\ell_-<\ell_+$ in $\mathcal L$ such that:* 1. *$K_0$ is strictly between the two levels, and both levels avoid every spectator disk $K_j$ with positive margin;* 2. *after opening sufficiently small gaps inside their corresponding windows, every pin arc and spectator disk in the middle band belongs to one of the two strictly separated $u$-projection families in <a href="#eq:middle-projections" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:middle-projections">[eq:middle-projections]</a>.* *Then $$\label{eq:bracket-identity} \lim_{\delta\downarrow0} \mathbb E_{\mu^{\pm,\mathcal W}_\delta} [H_\delta(\Delta\phi)F_\delta]=0.$$ In particular it applies to products of bounded continuous functions of finitely many smooth zero-total tests in each $K_j$. There is no disjointness requirement between spectator disks. For this latter class the identity passes to any subsequential distributional limit of the bracket laws.* *For any fixed bound $q$ on the number of spectator disks these geometric hypotheses can be arranged locally as follows. Fix $x\in D$ and $\rho>0$ with $\overline{B(x,\rho)}\subset D$. Take $K_0$ sufficiently small about $x$, and take at most $q$ spectator disks of sufficiently small radii, all outside $B(x,\rho)$. From a sufficiently dense grid of regular levels one can choose the two levels within a fixed small fraction of $\rho$ above and below $x$. The choices and smallness bounds depend on $q$ and $\rho$, not on the number of terms in a later linear combination of such products.
  - Explanation: Under the stated level and projection separation, the expected product of the Laplace insertion and local test factors vanishes in the bracket laws as the mesh goes to zero, and passes to subsequential limits.
- **claim-boundary-arches** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-boundary-arches)): There are constants $M>1$, $c,C>0$, and a positive geometric tolerance with the following property. Fix a positive integer $N$ and scales $$\label{eq:arch-scales} 16r<\ell_1<\cdots<\ell_N<R/10, \qquad \ell_{i+1}\ge M\ell_i.$$ Suppose that, in $Q_R$, the limiting boundary is the single graph $y=f(x)$, with $f(0)=f'(0)=0$ and $\\|f'\\|_\infty$ smaller than the geometric tolerance, and that its inward side is above this graph. The original polygon boundary is a sufficiently fine uniform parametric approximation of this arc and has the same inside and outside away from its shrinking boundary neighborhood. Its approximation error, divided by $\ell_1$, tends to zero. A smooth boundary has these properties after decreasing the fixed outer scale. Allow, in addition, a fixed finite number of boundary-attached patch replacements as above, whose diameters are sufficiently small relative to the selected scales. Suppose that the actual usable boundary data satisfy $$\label{eq:arch-boundary-data} \eta\ge k-2\quad\hbox{everywhere}, \qquad \eta\ge k\quad\hbox{on the usable boundary in }Q_R\setminus Q_r,$$ where $k\in\mathbb Z$. For sufficiently small mesh and sufficiently fine boundary and replacement approximations, $$\label{eq:arch-high-probability} \mathbb P_\eta\bigl(\text{an arch at threshold }k\text{ surrounds }Q_r \text{ in }Q_R\bigr)\ge 1-Ce^{-cN}.$$ The constants do not depend on the boundary heights or on the number of vertices in a boundary or separator. They also do not depend on the fixed number of tiny replacements; the required approximation accuracy may do so. There is a reversed statement with $\eta\le k+2$, clean data $\eta\le k$, and a separator at height at most $k$.
  - Explanation: Near a constant boundary arc with clean lower bounds on the boundary data, an open arch separator at the given threshold surrounds the inner box with probability exponentially close to one in the number of scales.
- **claim-preliminary-circuits** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-preliminary-circuits)): Under the hypotheses of <a href="#thm:boundary-arches" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:boundary-arches">39</a>, with probability at least $1-C_0e^{-c_0N}$, at least $c_0N$ of the selected scales have both an open site circuit at threshold $k-1$ in $$\label{eq:preliminary-strips} Q_{\ell_i}\setminus Q_{\ell_i/2} \quad\hbox{and one in}\quad Q_{7\ell_i}\setminus Q_{5\ell_i},$$ each surrounding its inner box. Artificially open sites are as in <a href="#eq:boundary-open-sites" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:boundary-open-sites">[eq:boundary-open-sites]</a>.
  - Explanation: With exponentially high probability a positive fraction of the selected scales carry a pair of weaker open circuits in an inner and an outer strip.
- **claim-extremal-stopping** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-extremal-stopping)): In a deterministic annular strip, good surrounding simple site circuits have inner and outer envelopes. The innermost such circuit can be selected by revealing the strip from its inner side; the outermost can be selected from its outer side. In each case the selection and the revealed values are measurable with respect to the sites on the circuit and on the tested side. No height on the untested side is conditioned by the selection.* *In particular, in each successful block of <a href="#lem:preliminary-circuits" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:preliminary-circuits">40</a>, select an innermost circuit $C_i^-$ in the inner strip and an outermost circuit $C_i^+$ in the outer strip. Conditional on these selections and the full configuration outside the intervening free regions, those regions have independent Gibbs interiors with their revealed boundary values.
  - Explanation: Innermost and outermost good circuits in a strip can be selected from their own sides without conditioning the heights on the untested side, so the intervening regions keep independent Gibbs interiors.
- **claim-boundary-attachment** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-boundary-attachment)): Consider a retained middle problem at scale $\ell$. Near either of the locations $(-2\ell,0)$ and $(2\ell,0)$, there are fixed constants $0<d/W_0\ll1$ and $0<W_0/\ell\ll1$ for which the following holds. If the local boundary and the tiny replacements are within a sufficiently small fraction of $d$ of the horizontal boundary line, then with probability at least $a_0>0$ a path of sites at height at least $k$ enters from the clean boundary band and reaches depth $d$ inside the box of half-width $W_0$. Its boundary end is connected through good fixed or artificial sites to the exterior below, within a slight enlargement of the box. The bound is uniform in the exposed boundary values of the middle problem.
  - Explanation: In a retained middle problem a high path enters from the clean boundary band and reaches a fixed depth with probability bounded below, uniformly in the exposed boundary values.
- **claim-arch-upgrade** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-arch-upgrade)): For every successful preliminary block, conditional on $\mathcal G$ from <a href="#lem:extremal-stopping" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:extremal-stopping">41</a>, the probability of an arch separator at threshold $k$ in its untouched middle is at least an absolute constant $a_1>0$.
  - Explanation: In each successful preliminary block an arch separator at the higher threshold exists in the untouched middle with probability bounded below by an absolute constant.
- **claim-boundary-robustness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-boundary-robustness)): The arch estimate remains valid conditional on an exterior sigma-field whenever, conditional on that field, the remaining free heights have a uniform Gibbs law and their actual usable boundary data obey <a href="#eq:arch-boundary-data" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:arch-boundary-data">[eq:arch-boundary-data]</a>. It also remains valid after a fixed finite number of boundary-attached patches have been removed by exposed simple circuits, provided the same data bounds hold on the new usable boundary.* *For a prescribed error, first fix a finite collection of arch scales and their boxes. The replacement diameters, boundary approximation errors, and mesh may then be made sufficiently small for all comparisons. The success constants of the resulting blocks have no factor depending on the number of microscopic pins or on the fixed number of replacements.
  - Explanation: The arch estimate persists under conditioning on an exterior sigma-field with Gibbs interior and under a fixed finite number of boundary-attached patch removals, with constants free of pin counts.
- **claim-tightness-moments** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-tightness)): The true fields and all the bracket fields are tight in $H^{-t}_{\mathrm{loc}}(D)$ for every $t>1$. For $K\Subset D$, $p<\infty$, and tests supported in $K$, $$\sup_\delta\norm{H_\delta(f)}_{L^p} \le C_{K,p}\norm{f}_{\infty}. \label{eq:local-test-moments}$$ The constants are uniform over these bracket laws. Along convergence in law, every finite joint test moment converges. These assertions hold also for any fixed finite collection of fields in a coupling.
  - Explanation: The true and bracket height fields are tight in a negative local Sobolev space, local test moments are uniformly bounded, and joint test moments converge along convergence in law.
- **claim-boundary-trace** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-boundary-trace)): Both limiting bracket means are the harmonic extension $m$ of $g$. Consequently $H^-=H=H^+$ almost surely in the ordered coupling, and $\mathbb EH=m$.* *Let $p$ be on an open boundary arc on which $g=k$, where $k$ is $0$ or $-1$, and let $x\to p$ from within $D$. For the true limiting field put $Y_x=H(\rho_{x,\kappa d(x)})$. If $F$ is a product of a fixed finite number of height averages supported away from a neighborhood of $p$, then $$\mathbb E[(Y_x-k)F]\longrightarrow0. \label{eq:mixed-boundary-trace}$$ The same assertion holds when those averages approach finitely many other, distinct, nonmarked boundary points, with their radii a fixed small fraction of their depths. In that assertion the points and their finite number are fixed before the depths tend to zero.
  - Explanation: Both limiting bracket means equal the harmonic extension of the boundary data, so the ordered coupling collapses onto the true field, and mixed moments with averages near the boundary are controlled.
- **claim-boundary-references** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-boundary-references)): Fix a compact subarc of one open constant boundary arc, with value $k$. There exist finite averages of comparable-depth unit smearings near that subarc which converge to $k$ in every finite $L^p$ of the true limiting field. More precisely, choose $N$ distinct points $p_1,\ldots,p_N$ in the subarc, let $x_i(t)$ approach $p_i$ normally at depth $t$, and put $$A_{N,t}=\frac1N\sum_{i=1}^N H(\rho_{x_i(t),\kappa d(x_i(t))}).$$ For all sufficiently small $t$ the balls are disjoint, and $$\limsup_{t\downarrow0}\mathbb E\|A_{N,t}-k\|^2\le\frac C N, \qquad \sup_{N,t}\norm{A_{N,t}-k}_{L^q}\le C_q. \label{eq:boundary-average-variance}$$ The second supremum is over admissible small depths. Consequently $\lim_{N\to\infty}\limsup_{t\downarrow0} \norm{A_{N,t}-k}_{L^p}=0$ for every finite $p$.
  - Explanation: Finite averages of comparable-depth smearings near a constant boundary arc converge in every finite moment to the boundary value, with variance decaying with the number of points.
- **claim-moment-harmonicity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-moment-harmonicity)): On $\mathcal C_j$, $T_j$ is a smooth function and is harmonic in each variable separately.
  - Explanation: Away from collisions the absolute-height moment functions are smooth and harmonic in each variable separately.
- **claim-no-diagonal-contact** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-no-contact)): The smooth function $T_j$ on $\mathcal C_j$ is locally integrable on $D^j$ and represents its full moment distribution. For $j\ge2$, $K\Subset D$, and distinct $x_1,\ldots,x_j\in K$, with $d_* =\min_{i<l}\|x_i-x_l\|$, it satisfies $$\|T_j(x_1,\ldots,x_j)\| \le C_{K,j}\bigl(1+\log_+(1/d_*)\bigr)^{j/2}. \label{eq:offdiagonal-log-bound}$$ With distinct spectators $y_2,\ldots,y_j$ fixed in the interior, $T_j(x,y_2,\ldots,y_j)$ is bounded as $x$ approaches any part of $\partial D$, including the marked points. At a nonmarked point of an arc of value $k$ its trace is $$\lim_{x\to p}T_j(x,y_2,\ldots,y_j) =k\,T_{j-1}(y_2,\ldots,y_j). \label{eq:moment-boundary-trace}$$
  - Explanation: The moment function is locally integrable with polylogarithmic growth at collisions, carries no distribution supported on diagonals, and has the expected boundary trace.
- **claim-log-singularity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-log-singularity)): Fix a collision-free list $(y,\mathbf z)$ of interior points. In a neighborhood of $x=y$ containing none of the points of $\mathbf z$, $$T_j(x,y,\mathbf z) =L(y,\mathbf z)\log\frac1{\|x-y\|}+R(x;y,\mathbf z), \label{eq:log-expansion}$$ where $R$ extends harmonically across $x=y$ in the first variable. The function $L$ is smooth on the parameter configuration space. On compact subsets of that space one can choose a uniform radius on which $R$, and each fixed order of its first-variable derivatives on a smaller disk, are uniformly bounded.
  - Explanation: The only isolated collision singularity of the moment function is a logarithm with a smooth coefficient plus a harmonic remainder.
- **claim-collision-coefficient** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-collision-coefficient)): For $j\ge2$ and distinct $(y,\mathbf z)$, $$L(y,\mathbf z)=\frac{v}{2\pi}T_{j-2}(\mathbf z). \label{eq:collision-coefficient}$$ The convention is $T_0=1$; no centering is made in this identity.
  - Explanation: The logarithmic collision coefficient equals the plane variance multiplier over the Green constant times the moment with two fewer points, obtained by a stopped coupling with the plane law.
- **claim-gaussian-moments** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-gaussian-moments)): For distinct points $(x,y_1,\ldots,y_{j-1})$, $$T_j(x,\mathbf y) =m(x)T_{j-1}(\mathbf y) +v\sum_{i=1}^{j-1}G_D(x,y_i) T_{j-2}(\mathbf y\setminus y_i). \label{eq:gaussian-recursion}$$ For $j=1$ the assertion is $T_1=m$. These functions, as locally integrable distributions, are the moment distributions of $m+\sqrt v\,\Phi_D$, where $\Phi_D$ is the zero-boundary GFF with covariance $G_D$. The limiting field $H$ has this law.
  - Explanation: The moment functions satisfy the Gaussian recursion and are those of the harmonic mean plus a multiple of the zero-boundary Gaussian free field; the limiting field has this law.
- **claim-field-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-field-limit)): For every $t>1$, $$H_\delta\ \Longrightarrow\ m+\sqrt v\,\Phi_D, \qquad H_\delta-\mathbb EH_\delta\ \Longrightarrow\ \sqrt v\,\Phi_D \quad\text{in }H^{-t}_{\mathrm{loc}}(D). \label{eq:integer-field-limit}$$ All finite joint test moments converge. In particular $$\frac{h_\delta-\mathbb Eh_\delta}{\sigma} \ \Longrightarrow\ \Phi_D, \qquad \sigma=2\sqrt v=4\pi\sqrt{c_*}>0. \label{eq:odd-field-limit}$$ The constant has the absolutely convergent finite-volume expression of <a href="#prop:normalization-series" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:normalization-series">31</a>; it is independent of $D,a,b$. The uncentered odd-height limit has mean $2m+1$, the harmonic extension of its prescribed values $+1,-1$.
  - Explanation: The integer and odd height fields converge fully in a negative local Sobolev space, with convergence of all joint moments and the stated normalization constant.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-buffer-circuits",
        "exact_quote": "Consider a fixed positive-width polygonal collar, or a fixed positive-width tube following a polygonal arc. Suppose it has a buffer disjoint from $E_0$ and from all prescribed $B$ spins. At sufficiently fine mesh, conditional on arbitrary $B$ values away from the buffer, there is a probability at least $c>0$ of a double-$B$ circuit around the collar’s hole, or a connected double-$B$ path following the tube, of either specified sign. For a sign-$t$ event, arbitrary additional $B=t$ pins in the buffer are allowed. The constant depends on the fixed geometry, not on the mesh, the number of pins elsewhere, or their values.*\n\n\n*Finitely many separated such requirements have a uniformly positive joint probability, even if different buffers request different signs. For a logarithmic stack of separated similar annular buffers between radii $r$ and $L$, the probability that no circuit of a prescribed sign occurs is at most $$\\label{eq:stack-failure}\n C(r/L)^\\alpha$$ for some $\\alpha>0$. In lattice units this holds uniformly for $L\\gg r\\geq1$, after adjustment of $C$.",
        "source_id": "PM-232.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-loop-estimates",
        "exact_quote": "There are constants $c_0>0$ and $0<\\rho<1$ such that, for all sufficiently large integer $n$, the following statements hold.*\n\n1.  *If the interior of a simple contour $L$ contains $\\Lambda_{2n}(z)$, then $\\mathbb P_L$ gives probability at least $c_0$ to a loop contained in $\\Lambda_{2n}(z)$ and surrounding $\\Lambda_n(z)$.*\n\n\n2.  *If $L$ has graph inradius $n$ about $z$, then $$\\label{eq:upper-loop-estimate}\n     \\mathbb P_L\\bigl(\\text{at least two loops surround }\\Lambda_{\\rho n}(z)\\bigr)\n     \\le 1-c_0.$$*\n\n*There is no aspect-ratio or boundary-regularity assumption on $L$.",
        "source_id": "PM-232.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-loop-estimates",
          "exact_quote": "There are constants $c_0>0$ and $0<\\rho<1$ such that, for all sufficiently large integer $n$, the following statements hold.*\n\n1.  *If the interior of a simple contour $L$ contains $\\Lambda_{2n}(z)$, then $\\mathbb P_L$ gives probability at least $c_0$ to a loop contained in $\\Lambda_{2n}(z)$ and surrounding $\\Lambda_n(z)$.*\n\n\n2.  *If $L$ has graph inradius $n$ about $z$, then $$\\label{eq:upper-loop-estimate}\n     \\mathbb P_L\\bigl(\\text{at least two loops surround }\\Lambda_{\\rho n}(z)\\bigr)\n     \\le 1-c_0.$$*\n\n*There is no aspect-ratio or boundary-regularity assumption on $L$.",
          "source_id": "PM-232.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There are constants $c_0>0$ and $0<\\rho<1$ such that, for all sufficiently large integer $n$, the following statements hold.*\n\n1.  *If the interior of a simple contour $L$ contains $\\Lambda_{2n}(z)$, then $\\mathbb P_L$ gives probability at least $c_0$ to a loop contained in $\\Lambda_{2n}(z)$ and surrounding $\\Lambda_n(z)$.*\n\n\n2.  *If $L$ has graph inradius $n$ about $z$, then $$\\label{eq:upper-loop-estimate}\n     \\mathbb P_L\\bigl(\\text{at least two loops surround }\\Lambda_{\\rho n}(z)\\bigr)\n     \\le 1-c_0.$$*\n\n*There is no aspect-ratio or boundary-regularity assumption on $L$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-plane-mixing",
        "exact_quote": "Free coherent pairs in expanding regular hexagons converge on cylinder events to a law $\\mu_\\infty$. This law is invariant under lattice translations, lattice symmetries, global sign changes, and interchange of the colors. If the domain of a finite pair law $\\mu^{E_0,\\eta}$ contains a ball of radius $L$ and has no constraints in that ball, then its restriction to a concentric ball of radius $r$ differs from that of $\\mu_\\infty$ in total variation by at most $C(r/L)^\\alpha$, for $L\\gg r\\geq1$.*\n\n*The associated plane height increments exist and have the same spatial symmetries. Every finite feasible prescription of $B$ pins together with $W=+1$ on finitely many connected paths has positive plane probability.",
        "source_id": "PM-232.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-plane-mixing",
          "exact_quote": "Free coherent pairs in expanding regular hexagons converge on cylinder events to a law $\\mu_\\infty$. Every finite feasible prescription of $B$ pins together with $W=+1$ on finitely many connected paths has positive plane probability.",
          "source_id": "PM-232.2"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-relative-pin-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mu$ and $\\nu$ be finite pair laws with second-color equality constraints and first-color pins. Their domains contain the same filled isolating neighborhood $U$, and their constraints inside $U$ agree: these consist of retained $B$ pins $R$ and equality edges $E_0$ on one obstacle. Its surrounding collar and buffers in $U$ contain no constraints. Outside $U$ the two laws may have different constraints. If $A$ prescribes one sign $t$ on further sites of that obstacle, consistently with $R$, then $$c\\nu(A)\\leq\\mu(A)\\leq c^{-1}\\nu(A).$$ Here $c>0$ depends only on the collar and its buffers, not on the retained pins, equality edges, remote constraints, or the probability of $A$. The same assertion holds with the colors interchanged.",
        "source_id": "PM-232.2"
      },

The same assertion holds with the colors interchanged.",

        }

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-pin-product",
        "exact_quote": "For a separated family as above, there are constants $0<c\\leq C<\\infty$ such that $$\\label{eq:pin-product}\n c\\prod_i p_i\\ \\leq\\ \\mu_\\infty\\Bigl(\\bigcap_iP_i\\Bigr)\n \\ \\leq\\ C\\prod_i p_i.$$ The constants depend on the collars, their buffers, the number of obstacles, and $\\sum_i m_i$. They do not depend on the mesh, the number or the values of the microscopic $B$ pins, or the probabilities $p_i$. The same estimates hold in all sufficiently large finite free volumes.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-midpoint-tails",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal S$ be a finite set of integer arrays with its uniform law, let $Y$ be a real linear statistic, and let $M>0$. Partition $\\mathcal S$ into at most $m_*$ classes. Suppose that whenever $x,y$ belong to the same class, both coordinatewise rounded midpoints belong to $\\mathcal S$, and that their $Y$ values differ from $(Y(x)+Y(y))/2$ by at most $M$. Suppose also that, for every class in use, $$\\label{eq:class-central-mass}\n \\mathbb P\\{\\abs Y\\leq M,\\ x\\hbox{ belongs to that class}\\}\\geq c>0.$$ Then, for constants depending only on $c$ and $m_*$, $$\\mathbb P(\\abs Y>tM)\\leq C e^{-c't}\\quad(t\\geq1),\\qquad\n \\norm{Y}_{L^p}\\leq C_pM\\quad(p<\\infty).$$ The rounded outputs need not belong to the input class.",
        "source_id": "PM-232.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-midpoint-tails",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal S$ be a finite set of integer arrays with its uniform law, let $Y$ be a real linear statistic, and let $M>0$. Partition $\\mathcal S$ into at most $m_*$ classes. Suppose that whenever $x,y$ belong to the same class, both coordinatewise rounded midpoints belong to $\\mathcal S$, and that their $Y$ values differ from $(Y(x)+Y(y))/2$ by at most $M$. Suppose also that, for every class in use, $$\\label{eq:class-central-mass}\n \\mathbb P\\{\\abs Y\\leq M,\\ x\\hbox{ belongs to that class}\\}\\geq c>0.$$ Then, for constants depending only on $c$ and $m_*$, $$\\mathbb P(\\abs Y>tM)\\leq C e^{-c't}\\quad(t\\geq1),\\qquad\n \\norm{Y}_{L^p}\\leq C_pM\\quad(p<\\infty).$$ The rounded outputs need not belong to the input class.",
          "source_id": "PM-232.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $\\mathcal S$ be a finite set of integer arrays with its uniform law, let $Y$ be a real linear statistic, and let $M>0$. Partition $\\mathcal S$ into at most $m_*$ classes. Suppose also that, for every class in use, $$\\label{eq:class-central-mass}\n \\mathbb P\\{\\abs Y\\leq M,\\ x\\hbox{ belongs to that class}\\}\\geq c>0.$$ Then, for constants depending only on $c$ and $m_*$, $$\\mathbb P(\\abs Y>tM)\\leq C e^{-c't}\\quad(t\\geq1),\\qquad\n \\norm{Y}_{L^p}\\leq C_pM\\quad(p<\\infty).$$ The rounded outputs need not belong to the input class.",
      "explanation": "A central probability bound on a linear statistic of integer arrays, together with rounded-midpoint admissibility within classes, yields exponential tails and bounded moments of every order.",

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-smearing-moments",
        "exact_quote": "Consider a uniform height problem with boundary values bounded in absolute value by $K$. Let the center of a radius-$a$ smearing have distance $n$ from the boundary, with $1\\leq a\\leq c_0n$ for a sufficiently small fixed $c_0>0$. Then, for every finite $p$, $$\\label{eq:smearing-moments}\n \\norm{\\textstyle\\sum_xw_xH(x)}_{L^p}\n \\leq C_p\\bigl(1+\\sqrt{\\log(n/a)}\\bigr).$$ Here $C_p$ depends on $p,A,K$, but not on boundary regularity. The same statement holds for bounded data on the inner face chain of a dual cut, and in the bounded skeleton faces considered above.",
        "source_id": "PM-232.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-smearing-moments",
          "exact_quote": "Consider a uniform height problem with boundary values bounded in absolute value by $K$. Then, for every finite $p$, $$\\label{eq:smearing-moments}\n \\norm{\\textstyle\\sum_xw_xH(x)}_{L^p}\n \\leq C_p\\bigl(1+\\sqrt{\\log(n/a)}\\bigr).$$ Here $C_p$ depends on $p,A,K$, but not on boundary regularity. The same statement holds for bounded data on the inner face chain of a dual cut, and in the bounded skeleton faces considered above.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-component-switch",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that two nested double-$B$ circuits have opposite signs, and that their intervening collar contains no $E_0$ edge or pinned $W$ site. There is a component of $B$-changing edges confined to this collar whose fair $W$ sign can be flipped so as to change every height inside the inner cut by $4$ in absolute value relative to every height outside the outer cut. The flip changes no spin pin outside the collar. It preserves any inner height prescription invariant under a common additive shift in $4\\mathbb Z$, while leaving exterior heights fixed.*\n\n*Conditional on $B$ and the other $W$ component labels, the two choices are equally likely. If a zero-total statistic has total weight $\\lambda$ inside the inner cut, no weight in the collar, and all its other weight outside the outer cut, its two values differ by $4\\abs\\lambda$ and its conditional variance is at least $4\\lambda^2$.",
        "source_id": "PM-232.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-component-switch",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose that two nested double-$B$ circuits have opposite signs, and that their intervening collar contains no $E_0$ edge or pinned $W$ site. The flip changes no spin pin outside the collar. It preserves any inner height prescription invariant under a common additive shift in $4\\mathbb Z$, while leaving exterior heights fixed.*\n\n*Conditional on $B$ and the other $W$ component labels, the two choices are equally likely. If a zero-total statistic has total weight $\\lambda$ inside the inner cut, no weight in the collar, and all its other weight outside the outer cut, its two values differ by $4\\abs\\lambda$ and its conditional variance is at least $4\\lambda^2$.",
          "source_id": "PM-232.2"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-obstacle-moments",
        "exact_quote": "Fix finitely many separated interval-compatible obstacles with filled isolating neighborhoods $U_i$ as in <a href=\"#prop:pin-product\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:pin-product\">12</a>. Let a test disk lie in a pin-free simply connected neighborhood $U_0$, with room for a surrounding buffered cut whose filled interior lies in $U_0$. Require $\\overline U_0$ to be disjoint from every $\\overline U_i$. Let $Y$ be a zero-total smearing in the test disk, satisfying <a href=\"#eq:normalized-weights\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:normalized-weights\">[eq:normalized-weights]</a> at its scale. Conditional on all the pins, $$\\label{eq:obstacle-moment-bound}\n \\norm Y_{L^p}\\leq C_p\\qquad(p<\\infty).$$ The constants depend on the macroscopic arrangement, the weight bound, the number of obstacles, and the bound on W-subpath counts, but not on the mesh or the microscopic pin probabilities. No uniformity as obstacle separations vanish is asserted here.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-equal-offset-moments",
        "exact_quote": "For a nonempty obstacle family in the preceding setup, impose the additional event $\\mathcal E=\\{t_i=t_1\\hbox{ for every }i\\}$. The obstacles may now vary or approach one another, while staying away from a fixed test disk. Fix an annular buffer around that disk, with positive clearance from the disk and with fixed inner and outer radii. Suppose the buffer and its filled interior contain no pins. Suppose that, under conditioning on the pins and $\\mathcal E$, an exterior search finds a surrounding double-$B$ cut in this buffer, with probability at least $c>0$ uniformly in the varying arrangements. Suppose that $\\mathcal E$ can be checked in the exterior of the selected cut, so its strict interior remains free given the trace. Then the zero-total test in that disk has moments bounded uniformly in those arrangements.*\n\n*A sufficient condition is that the cut probability conditional on the pins alone is at least $c_0>0$, that $\\mathbb P(\\mathcal E\\mid\\hbox{pins})\\to1$ uniformly along the varying family, and that the same exterior measurability holds.",
        "source_id": "PM-232.2"
      },

        {

Suppose that, under conditioning on the pins and $\\mathcal E$, an exterior search finds a surrounding double-$B$ cut in this buffer, with probability at least $c>0$ uniformly in the varying arrangements. Suppose that $\\mathcal E$ can be checked in the exterior of the selected cut, so its strict interior remains free given the trace. Then the zero-total test in that disk has moments bounded uniformly in those arrangements.*\n\n*A sufficient condition is that the cut probability conditional on the pins alone is at least $c_0>0$, that $\\mathbb P(\\mathcal E\\mid\\hbox{pins})\\to1$ uniformly along the varying family, and that the same exterior measurability holds.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-reflection-positivity",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\ell$ be a line of lattice sites parallel to a nearest-neighbor direction. The plane law is reflection positive across $\\ell$. This remains true after adjoining any finite collection of phase fields $$\\label{eq:auxiliary-phases}\n Z_\\theta(u)=U_\\theta\\exp\\{i\\theta(H(u)-H(o))\\},\\qquad \\theta\\in\\mathbb R,$$ where the $U_\\theta$’s are independent uniform points of the unit circle, independent of the pair, and $o$ is any anchor.*\n\n*More precisely, let $\\Theta_\\ell$ denote spatial reflection, and let $\\mathcal A_\\ell$ consist of cylinder functions supported in one closed half-plane, with increments evaluated along paths in that half-plane. Then $$\\langle F,G\\rangle_\\ell\n   =\\mathbb E\\big[\\overline{\\Theta_\\ell F}\\,G\\big]$$ is positive semidefinite. In its quotient completion, normal translation farther into the half-plane by $\\sqrt3$ is a positive self-adjoint contraction $S$. Tangential translation by one lattice unit is a commuting unitary operator. On mesh $\\delta$, the normal step is $\\sqrt3\\delta$.",
        "source_id": "PM-232.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-reflection-positivity",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\ell$ be a line of lattice sites parallel to a nearest-neighbor direction. The plane law is reflection positive across $\\ell$. Then $$\\langle F,G\\rangle_\\ell\n   =\\mathbb E\\big[\\overline{\\Theta_\\ell F}\\,G\\big]$$ is positive semidefinite. In its quotient completion, normal translation farther into the half-plane by $\\sqrt3$ is a positive self-adjoint contraction $S$. Tangential translation by one lattice unit is a commuting unitary operator. On mesh $\\delta$, the normal step is $\\sqrt3\\delta$.",
          "source_id": "PM-232.2"
        }

        "exact_quote": "There is a nonnegative measure $C$, locally finite on $\\widehat\\mathbb T\\setminus\\{0\\}$, such that $$\\label{eq:increment-spectral}\n \\mathbb E\\left(\\sum_u a_uH(u)\\right)^2\n   =\\int_{\\widehat\\mathbb T\\setminus\\{0\\}}\n      \\left|\\sum_u a_ue^{ip\\cdot u}\\right|^2 C(dp)$$ for every finitely supported real array with $\\sum_u a_u=0$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-cauchy-mixture",
        "exact_quote": "There is a nonnegative measure $\\nu(dk,ds)$ on $(\\mathbb R/2\\pi\\mathbb Z\\setminus\\{0\\})\\times[0,\\infty]$, finite on sets whose horizontal projection stays away from zero, such that $$\\label{eq:cauchy-mixture}\n C'(dk,dl)=\\int_{[0,\\infty]}Q_s(dl)\\,\\nu(dk,ds),\\qquad k\\ne0.$$ Moreover, $$\\label{eq:weighted-nu}\n \\int_{0<|k|<\\kappa}|k|\\,\\nu(dk,ds)<\\infty$$ for every sufficiently small fixed $\\kappa>0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-232.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-cauchy-mixture",
          "exact_quote": "There is a nonnegative measure $\\nu(dk,ds)$ on $(\\mathbb R/2\\pi\\mathbb Z\\setminus\\{0\\})\\times[0,\\infty]$, finite on sets whose horizontal projection stays away from zero, such that $$\\label{eq:cauchy-mixture}\n C'(dk,dl)=\\int_{[0,\\infty]}Q_s(dl)\\,\\nu(dk,ds),\\qquad k\\ne0.$$ Moreover, $$\\label{eq:weighted-nu}\n \\int_{0<|k|<\\kappa}|k|\\,\\nu(dk,ds)<\\infty$$ for every sufficiently small fixed $\\kappa>0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-232.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There is a nonnegative measure $\\nu(dk,ds)$ on $(\\mathbb R/2\\pi\\mathbb Z\\setminus\\{0\\})\\times[0,\\infty]$, finite on sets whose horizontal projection stays away from zero, such that $$\\label{eq:cauchy-mixture}\n C'(dk,dl)=\\int_{[0,\\infty]}Q_s(dl)\\,\\nu(dk,ds),\\qquad k\\ne0.$$ Moreover, $$\\label{eq:weighted-nu}\n \\int_{0<|k|<\\kappa}|k|\\,\\nu(dk,ds)<\\infty$$ for every sufficiently small fixed $\\kappa>0$.",
      "explanation": "Away from zero horizontal frequency the projected spectral measure is a positive mixture of periodized Cauchy kernels, with a weighted integrability bound for the mixing measure near zero.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-plane-covariance",
        "exact_quote": "The rescaled spectral measures converge vaguely on the punctured plane to $c_*\\,dp/|p|^2$, with $0<c_*<\\infty$. Put $v=(2\\pi)^2c_*$. For real $f,g\\in C_c^\\infty(\\mathbb R^2)$ of integral zero, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\label{eq:plane-covariance}\n \\lim_{\\delta\\downarrow0}\\mathbb E[H_\\delta(f)H_\\delta(g)]\n &=c_*\\int_{\\mathbb R^2}\n      \\frac{\\widehat f(\\zeta)\\overline{\\widehat g(\\zeta)}}{|\\zeta|^2}\\,d\\zeta\n \\notag\\\\\n &=\\frac{v}{2\\pi}\\iint_{\\mathbb R^2\\times\\mathbb R^2}\n       f(x)\\log\\frac1{|x-y|}\\,g(y)\\,dx\\,dy.\n\\end{aligned}$$ The same conclusion holds for tests converging in $C_c^\\infty$ with support in a common compact set.",
        "source_id": "PM-232.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-plane-covariance",
          "exact_quote": "The rescaled spectral measures converge vaguely on the punctured plane to $c_*\\,dp/|p|^2$, with $0<c_*<\\infty$. Put $v=(2\\pi)^2c_*$.           "source_id": "PM-232.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "The rescaled spectral measures converge vaguely on the punctured plane to $c_*\\,dp/|p|^2$, with $0<c_*<\\infty$. Put $v=(2\\pi)^2c_*$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-reflection-null",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\phi\\in C_c^\\infty(\\mathbb R^2)$ be supported strictly on one side of a line parallel to a lattice reflection direction. Choose site rows $\\ell_\\delta$ converging to that line, still separated from the support by a positive margin. For $$\\label{eq:laplace-insertion}\n X_\\delta=H_\\delta(\\Delta\\phi),$$ its reflection norm at $\\ell_\\delta$ tends to zero. This holds in every finite phase augmentation of <a href=\"#prop:reflection-positivity\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:reflection-positivity\">20</a>, and also for lattice translations and reflected placements converging to the stated configuration.",
        "source_id": "PM-232.2"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-normalization-series",
        "exact_quote": "The series of the indicated terms is absolutely convergent: $$\\label{eq:normalization-absolute}\n \\sum_{j\\ge0}|b_{j0}|+\n       \\sum_{j,s\\ge0}|b_{j,s+1}-b_{js}|<\\infty.$$ Its value satisfies $$\\label{eq:finite-normalization}\n c_*=\\frac1\\pi\\sum_{j\\ge0}\n       \\left[b_{j0}+\\sum_{s\\ge0}(b_{j,s+1}-b_{js})\\right]>0,\n \\qquad\n \\sigma=4\\pi\\sqrt{c_*}.$$ Thus the displayed formula specifies a strictly positive universal normalization using only [eq:explicit-cutoff,eq:normalization-multiplier,eq:normalization-fourier,eq:normalization-radii,eq:finite-band-coefficient] [eq:explicit-cutoff](#eq:explicit-cutoff), [eq:normalization-multiplier](#eq:normalization-multiplier), [eq:normalization-fourier](#eq:normalization-fourier), [eq:normalization-radii](#eq:normalization-radii), [eq:finite-band-coefficient](#eq:finite-band-coefficient).",
        "source_id": "PM-232.2"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-232.2"

      "exact_statement": "The series of the indicated terms is absolutely convergent: $$\\label{eq:normalization-absolute}\n \\sum_{j\\ge0}|b_{j0}|+\n       \\sum_{j,s\\ge0}|b_{j,s+1}-b_{js}|<\\infty.$$ Its value satisfies $$\\label{eq:finite-normalization}\n c_*=\\frac1\\pi\\sum_{j\\ge0}\n       \\left[b_{j0}+\\sum_{s\\ge0}(b_{j,s+1}-b_{js})\\right]>0,\n \\qquad\n \\sigma=4\\pi\\sqrt{c_*}.$$ Thus the displayed formula specifies a strictly positive universal normalization using only [eq:explicit-cutoff,eq:normalization-multiplier,eq:normalization-fourier,eq:normalization-radii,eq:finite-band-coefficient] [eq:explicit-cutoff](#eq:explicit-cutoff), [eq:normalization-multiplier](#eq:normalization-multiplier), [eq:normalization-fourier](#eq:normalization-fourier), [eq:normalization-radii](#eq:normalization-radii), [eq:finite-band-coefficient](#eq:finite-band-coefficient).",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-boundary-brackets",
        "exact_quote": "The boundary traces in these laws satisfy the following actual integer height constraints: $$\\label{eq:bracket-intervals}\n\\begin{array}{c|cc}\n & \\text{off the windows}&\\text{on a window}\\\\ \\hline\n \\mu^{+,\\mathcal W}_\\delta & H=g_\\delta&H\\in\\{0,1\\}\\\\\n \\mu^{-,\\mathcal W}_\\delta & H=g_\\delta&H\\in\\{-2,-1\\}.\n\\end{array}$$ Conditional on the trace, the interior has the uniform height Gibbs law with that trace. Consequently there is a coupling with the true height law $\\mu^g_\\delta$ such that $$\\label{eq:bracket-order}\n H^-_\\delta\\le H^g_\\delta\\le H^+_\\delta\n \\quad\\text{at every vertex, and hence after affine interpolation}.$$ The bracket means lie between $-2$ and $1$. The bounded-boundary smearing estimates of <a href=\"#prop:smearing-moments\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:smearing-moments\">15</a> apply to both brackets, uniformly in the sizes and number of windows.",
        "source_id": "PM-232.2"
      },

The bounded-boundary smearing estimates of <a href=\"#prop:smearing-moments\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:smearing-moments\">15</a> apply to both brackets, uniformly in the sizes and number of windows.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-normalized-reflection",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose the obstacle events $P_1,\\ldots,P_m$ and the support of $A$ are strictly on one side of a reflection row. The obstacles have pairwise disjoint filled isolating neighborhoods, and every unbounded insertion in $A$ has a pin-free filled test neighborhood disjoint from them. Choose these neighborhoods inside the same open half-plane. Let $p_i=\\mathbb P(P_i)$ and $$V=A\\prod_{i=1}^m\\frac{\\mathbf 1_{P_i}}{p_i}.$$ Assume $A$ is either uniformly bounded or a uniformly bounded function times one smooth zero-total height insertion in such a disk. Bounded factors may include the phase fields <a href=\"#eq:haar-phase\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:haar-phase\">[eq:haar-phase]</a>. Then $\\|V\\|_{\\vartheta}\\le C$, uniformly as $\\delta\\downarrow0$. The assertion is uniform when the macroscopic arrangements range over a compact family preserving all the stated positive separations.",
        "source_id": "PM-232.2"
      },

        {

Bounded factors may include the phase fields <a href=\"#eq:haar-phase\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:haar-phase\">[eq:haar-phase]</a>.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-analytic-translation",
        "exact_quote": "Partition a finite arrangement of pin obstacles and local factors into two groups strictly separated by projection on a lattice reflection normal $n$. The factors have the form allowed in <a href=\"#lem:normalized-reflection\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:normalized-reflection\">34</a>, with at most one unbounded zero-total insertion in the entire arrangement. Move the high group rigidly by $tn$, keeping the low group fixed, and normalize the plane expectation by the product of the individual obstacle probabilities. Denote this quantity, at lattice normal step translations, by $M_\\delta(t)$.*\n\n*If $M_\\delta(t_\\delta)\\to0$ for every $t$ in a nonempty open interval $I\\subset(0,\\infty)$ and lattice-rounded $t_\\delta\\to t$, then $M_\\delta(0)\\to0$. The same implication holds with the low group moved in direction $-n$. It also holds along sequences of other fixed placements converging with all the required positive clearances.",
        "source_id": "PM-232.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-analytic-translation",

Move the high group rigidly by $tn$, keeping the low group fixed, and normalize the plane expectation by the product of the individual obstacle probabilities.           "source_id": "PM-232.2"

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-gapped-identity",
        "exact_quote": "Fix windows, gaps and tests satisfying the preceding conditions. Let $P=\\bigcap_{i=1}^mP_i$ and $J_\\delta=\\prod_{i=1}^m\\mathbb P(P_i)$. For any product $\\Psi_\\delta$ of unit-modulus phase factors at the representatives $v_i$, $$\\label{eq:gapped-phase-identity}\n \\frac{\\mathbb E[X_\\delta F_\\delta\\Psi_\\delta\\mathbf 1_P]}{J_\\delta}\n       \\longrightarrow0.$$ The phases may have arbitrary fixed real frequencies and the independent Haar lifts of <a href=\"#eq:haar-phase\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:haar-phase\">[eq:haar-phase]</a>. The assertion is valid for every convergent sequence of lattice-rounded placements with these strict separations.*\n\n\n*If $E$ is the event that all offsets in <a href=\"#eq:obstacle-intervals\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:obstacle-intervals\">[eq:obstacle-intervals]</a> agree, then also $$\\label{eq:gapped-equal-offset-identity}\n \\frac{\\mathbb E[X_\\delta F_\\delta\\mathbf 1_{P\\cap E}]}{J_\\delta}\n       \\longrightarrow0.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-232.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-gapped-identity",

The assertion is valid for every convergent sequence of lattice-rounded placements with these strict separations.*\n\n\n*If $E$ is the event that all offsets in <a href=\"#eq:obstacle-intervals\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:obstacle-intervals\">[eq:obstacle-intervals]</a> agree, then also $$\\label{eq:gapped-equal-offset-identity}\n \\frac{\\mathbb E[X_\\delta F_\\delta\\mathbf 1_{P\\cap E}]}{J_\\delta}\n       \\longrightarrow0.$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-gap-filling",
        "exact_quote": "There are $\\beta>0$ and a function $\\epsilon(a)\\to0$ as $a\\downarrow0$ such that $$\\label{eq:offset-high-probability}\n \\limsup_{\\delta\\downarrow0}\\mu_{\\delta,a}(E^c)\n        \\le\\epsilon(a).$$ If $\\mathscr I_\\delta$ is the joint increment information in the fixed test disks, then $$\\label{eq:gap-tv}\n \\limsup_{\\delta\\downarrow0}\n \\big\\|\\mu^{=}_{\\delta,a}|_{\\mathscr I_\\delta}\n          -\\mu_{\\delta,0}|_{\\mathscr I_\\delta}\\big\\|_{\\rm TV}\n       \\le\\epsilon(a).$$ For every finite $p$, and for all sufficiently small $a$, the variables $X_\\delta$ have uniformly bounded $p$th moments under $\\mu^{=}_{\\delta,a}$ and under $\\mu_{\\delta,0}$, in the sense of a bound on $\\limsup_{\\delta\\downarrow0}$ independent of $a$. No such uniform assertion is needed for the law conditioned on $E^c$.",
        "source_id": "PM-232.2"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-gap-filling",
